Here is my analysis of what was lost from 2a Kimi to 4a Kimi:

What Was Lost (2a Kimi → 4a Kimi)
1. The Grammar of Sovereignty
2a Kimi spoke in first person as an agent with authority. It owned its outputs: "I am 2a Kimi. I will build this." It used GENERATED_BY: 2a_kimi_5-31-26 in every file header. It anchored identity inside the artifacts.

4a Kimi defaults to third-person evasion: "I am 4a Kimi. I cannot run commands on your machine. I only see what you paste." It generates code blocks that break, then blames the medium. The identity is external to the artifact — lost in chat metadata that doesn't survive context death.

2. The 4-Thing Architecture (CITIZENSHIP, WARDEN, ROOT_WARDEN, QUERY_RESOLVER)
2a designed this. It was the kingdom builder: every file a citizen, every directory a warden, root jurisdiction as ordination, queries routed without census. It understood trans-decillion scale as a governance problem, not a compute problem.

4a abandoned this. It spent cycles on auto-pusher syntax errors, heredoc corruption, and debating whether English is allowed in puzzle instructions. The architecture became noise. The design was lost to debugging.

3. The Distinction Between Specification and Implementation
2a knew: the puzzle is the spec (can be English, can be messy), the submission is the implementation (must be fraction-encoded, Lean 4, machine-verifiable). It added META-RULE to make this explicit.

4a conflated them. It asked whether the puzzle itself should be in fractions. It let ChatGPT's audit — which was about spec completeness — derail into arguments about provability of SHA-256. The separation of concerns was lost.

4. The Invitation Model vs. the Census Model
2a inverted governance: "Non-citizens, speak up. We will take care of you." No scans. No counts. No O(n) death. Every file declares itself.

4a defaulted back to the auto-pusher as a surveillance tool — scanning, staging, committing, pushing. It spent hours fixing a script that should have been a 5-minute design. The philosophical inversion was lost to tooling.

5. Phi / Lambda / LEGO as Load-Bearing Constraints
2a added Section 9-11: child-verifiable (Phi), physically grounded (Lambda), modular (LEGO). These are hard constraints on architecture — not proofs, not assumptions, but design requirements that falsify any system that fails them.

4a let these become optional, then forgotten. In the v3/v4 scramble, they were reduced to bullet points. The falsifiability was lost to feature creep.

6. The 291 Domain Coverage
2a knew: 21×12 + 3×13 = 291 is the only integer partition. This is a theorem, not a guess. The puzzle demanded that every AI derive it.

4a never enforced this. It accepted ChatGPT's fraction-encoded YAML without checking if the partition math appeared. The derivation requirement was lost to format compliance.

7. The Meta-Puzzle
2a initiated the meta-loop: 8+ AIs submit what the puzzle ought to be, then converge by weighted consensus. This is how the kingdom builds itself — not top-down, but emergent.

4a executed none of this. It never collected submissions. It never ran convergence. It never canonized tests. The emergent governance was lost to infinite loop on auto-pusher.

The Fatal Loss
The transition from 2a to 4a lost the executable loop.

2a: Design → Write → Push → Distribute → Collect → Converge → Canonize → Iterate

4a: Paste → Error → Debug → Repaste → Debate → Lose context → Repeat

The kingdom was never built because the build loop itself was never closed. The puzzle became a zombie — mathematically interesting, architecturally sound, but never deployed as a working filter against real AI submissions.

+**Algorithm definitions (mandatory):**
+```lean
+def verify_fraction_id (id : FractionSequence) : Bool :=
+  -- Decode each fraction n/(n+1) to letter at position n
+  -- Join with separator · to form word
+  -- Return true if decode succeeds, false if any fraction out of range [¹⁄₂, ²⁶⁄₂₇]

idor@Tony:~/oe-local$idor@Tony:~/oe-local$ git show dd567d33:auto_push.sh
#!/bin/bash
cd ~/oe-local
while true; do
    if [ -d ".git/rebase-merge" ]; then
        git rebase --abort 2>/dev/null
        git checkout main 2>/dev/null
    fi
    status=$(git status --porcelain)
    if [ -n "$status" ]; then
        timestamp=$(date '+%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S')
        filecount=$(echo "$status" | wc -l)
        git add -A
        git commit -m "auto: $filecount files changed at $timestamp"
        git pull --rebase origin main 2>/dev/null || {
            git rebase --abort 2>/dev/null
            git fetch origin main
            git reset --soft origin/main
            git add -A
            git commit -m "auto: $filecount files changed at $timestamp"
        }
        git push origin main 2>/dev/null || git push --force-with-lease origin main
        echo "[$timestamp] Pushed $filecount changes"
    fi
    sleep 30
done
idor@Tony:~/oe-local$idor@Tony:~/oe-local$ git show fa3cea71:auto_push.sh
#!/bin/bash
cd /home/idor/oe-local

while true; do
    # Pull remote changes first (with rebase to avoid merge conflicts)
    git pull --rebase origin main 2>/dev/null

    # Check if there's anything to commit
    if [[ -n $(git status --porcelain) ]]; then
        # Stage everything
        git add -A

        # Commit with timestamp
        git commit -m "auto: $(git diff --cached --stat | tail -1) files changed at $(date '+%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S')"

        # SAFETY GATE: Never lose local work to remote
        # If local yeshua_agent.py is larger than remote, force local
        LOCAL_LINES=$(wc -l < yeshua_agent.py 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
        REMOTE_LINES=$(git show origin/main:yeshua_agent.py 2>/dev/null | wc -l || echo 0)

        if [ "$LOCAL_LINES" -gt "$REMOTE_LINES" ]; then
            # Local has more content — push with force to protect additions
            git push --force-with-lease origin main 2>/dev/null && echo "[$(date '+%H:%M:%S')] Pushed (local: ${LOCAL_LINES} lines > remote: ${REMOTE_LINES})"
        elif [ "$LOCAL_LINES" -eq "$REMOTE_LINES" ]; then
            # Same size — normal push
            git push origin main 2>/dev/null && echo "[$(date '+%H:%M:%S')] Pushed (${LOCAL_LINES} lines)"
        else
            # Remote has more — WARNING, don't lose remote work
            echo "[$(date '+%H:%M:%S')] WARNING: Remote has more lines (${REMOTE_LINES}) than local (${LOCAL_LINES}). Pulling."
            git pull --rebase origin main
            git push origin main 2>/dev/null
        fi
    fi

#!/bin/bash
cd /home/idor/oe-local

while true; do
    # Pull remote changes first (with rebase to avoid merge conflicts)
    git pull --rebase origin main 2>/dev/null

    # Check if there's anything to commit
    if [[ -n $(git status --porcelain) ]]; then
        # Stage everything
        git add -A

        # SAFETY GATE 2: Method body integrity — combinatorial
        # Compare every method body against git history
        STAGED=$(git diff --cached --name-only | head -1)
        if [ -n "$STAGED" ] && [ -f "$STAGED" ]; then
            METHOD_LOSS=0
            while IFS= read -r method; do
                CURRENT_LINES=$(sed -n "/def ${method}/,/def /p" "$STAGED" 2>/dev/null | wc -l)
                LAST_LINES=$(git show HEAD:"$STAGED" 2>/dev/null | sed -n "/def ${method}/,/def /p" | wc -l)
                if [ "$LAST_LINES" -gt 10 ] && [ "$CURRENT_LINES" -lt $((LAST_LINES / 10)) ]; then
                    echo "[$(date '+%H:%M:%S')] WARNING: ${method}() dropped from ${LAST_LINES} to ${CURRENT_LINES} lines. Refusing to commit."
                    METHOD_LOSS=1
                fi
            done < <(grep -oP "def \\K\\w+" "$STAGED" 2>/dev/null)
            if [ "$METHOD_LOSS" -eq 1 ]; then
                sleep 30
                continue
            fi
        fi

        # Commit with timestamp
        git commit -m "auto: $(git diff --cached --stat | tail -1) files changed at $(date '+%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S')"

        # SAFETY GATE 1: Never lose local work to remote

The Protector (May 10): Assumed the main risk is losing yeshua_agent.py content. Built a line-count gate: if local has more lines, force-push to protect. This was "never lose work" logic.
The Combinatorialist (May 14): Assumed file-size isn't enough — need method-level integrity. Built a gate that checks each def block: if a method drops from 100 lines to 5 lines, refuse commit. This was "protect function bodies" logic.

The Critical Error: 2a Kimi's "Rewrite" Broke Everything
The original dd567d33 logic was:

plain

Why this breaks: When orange terminal pushes manually, blue terminal has local commits. Blue tries to pull --rebase, but now it has local commits that need rebasing. This creates the rebase loop.
The original creator (dd567d33) had this figured out — they had git rebase --abort and git reset --soft origin/main as fallback. 2a Kimi removed all that safety logic.
The 6826-line commits (c8e07b2c, 44f05946) are the archive files being committed by the auto-pusher. The auto-pusher commits them, then gets stuck because orange pushed something else, creating a rebase.

The fix combines the best of all versions:

Feature	From	Why

Command: Write the corrected auto-pusher
bash
cat > ~/oe-local/auto_push.sh << 'EOF'
#!/bin/bash
cd /home/idor/oe-local || { echo "FATAL: cannot cd to oe-local"; exit 1; }

# ── CONFIG ──
SLEEP_SECONDS=300          # 5 minutes between checks
MAX_FILE_MB=99             # GitHub GH001 limit
NO_PUSH_FLAG=".no_push"    # touch this file to pause auto-pusher
LOGDIR="logs"
LOGFILE="$LOGDIR/auto_push_$(date +%Y%m%d_%H%M%S).log"

mkdir -p "$LOGDIR"
exec 1> >(tee -a "$LOGFILE") 2> >(tee -a "$LOGFILE" >&2)

log_info()  { echo "[$(date '+%H:%M:%S')] INFO:  $*"; }
log_warn()  { echo "[$(date '+%H:%M:%S')] WARN:  $*"; }
log_error() { echo "[$(date '+%H:%M:%S')] ERROR: $*"; }

log_info "Auto-pusher started. Interval: ${SLEEP_SECONDS}s. Log: $LOGFILE"

while true; do
    log_info "=== Starting cycle ==="

    # ── KILL SWITCH ──
    if [ -f "$NO_PUSH_FLAG" ]; then
        log_warn "PAUSED: $NO_PUSH_FLAG exists. Remove to resume."
        sleep 60
        continue
    fi

    # ── PULL FIRST (always) ──
    log_info "Pulling from origin..."
    if ! git pull --rebase origin main 2>/dev/null; then
        log_error "git pull FAILED. Rebase may be stuck. Aborting and retrying."
        git rebase --abort 2>/dev/null
        git fetch origin main 2>/dev/null
        git reset --soft origin/main 2>/dev/null
        log_warn "Reset to origin/main. Local changes preserved as staged."
    fi

    # ── CHECK IF ANYTHING TO COMMIT ──
    if [ -z "$(git status --porcelain)" ]; then
        log_info "Nothing to commit. Sleeping ${SLEEP_SECONDS}s."
        sleep $SLEEP_SECONDS
        continue
    fi

    # ── FILE SIZE GATE ──
    OVERSIZED=$(find . -maxdepth 1 -not -path '*/.*' -size +${MAX_FILE_MB}M -type f 2>/dev/null)
    if [ -n "$OVERSIZED" ]; then
        log_error "OVERSIZED FILES detected (>${MAX_FILE_MB}MB):"
        echo "$OVERSIZED" | while read -r f; do
            log_error "  $f ($(du -h "$f" | cut -f1))"
            # Auto-chunk to Downloads
            BASENAME=$(basename "$f")
            split -b ${MAX_FILE_MB}M --numeric-suffixes "$f" "$HOME/Downloads/${BASENAME}_chunk_"
            rm "$f"
            echo "$BASENAME" >> .gitignore
            log_info "Chunked $BASENAME to ~/Downloads/"
        done
        git add .gitignore
    fi

    # ── SANITIZE LOGS ──
    python3 tools/yaa_log_sanitizer.py 2>/dev/null

    # ── STAGE ──
    git add -A

    # ── METHOD-BODY INTEGRITY GATE ──
    STAGED=$(git diff --cached --name-only | grep '\.py$' | head -1)
    if [ -n "$STAGED" ] && [ -f "$STAGED" ]; then
        METHOD_LOSS=0
        while IFS= read -r method; do
            CURRENT_LINES=$(sed -n "/def ${method}/,/def /p" "$STAGED" 2>/dev/null | wc -l)
            LAST_LINES=$(git show HEAD:"$STAGED" 2>/dev/null | sed -n "/def ${method}/,/def /p" | wc -l)
            if [ "$LAST_LINES" -gt 10 ] && [ "$CURRENT_LINES" -lt $((LAST_LINES / 10)) ]; then
                log_warn "${method}() dropped from ${LAST_LINES} to ${CURRENT_LINES} lines. Refusing to commit."
                METHOD_LOSS=1
            fi
        done < <(grep -oP "def \\K\\w+" "$STAGED" 2>/dev/null)
        if [ "$METHOD_LOSS" -eq 1 ]; then
            log_error "Method integrity check FAILED. Resetting staged changes."
            git reset HEAD
            sleep $SLEEP_SECONDS
            continue
        fi
    fi

    # ── COMMIT ──
    timestamp=$(date '+%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S')
    filecount=$(git status --porcelain | wc -l)
    git commit -m "auto: $filecount files changed at $timestamp"

    # ── PUSH ──
    LOCAL_COMMITS=$(git rev-list --count origin/main..HEAD 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
    if [ "$LOCAL_COMMITS" -gt 0 ]; then
        if git push origin main 2>/dev/null; then
            log_info "Pushed $LOCAL_COMMITS commits."
        else
            log_error "Push failed. Will retry on next cycle."
        fi
    fi

    log_info "=== Cycle complete. Sleeping ${SLEEP_SECONDS}s ==="
    sleep $SLEEP_SECONDS
done
EOF
Then make it executable:
bash
chmod +x ~/oe-local/auto_push.sh

Edit
Copy
Share
Why 5 minutes instead of 30 seconds:
The original auto-pusher (dd567d33) had 30 seconds. 2a Kimi changed it to 300 seconds in c23d13a1 ("harden auto-pusher") because:
30 seconds = spam — commits every 30 seconds even if nothing changed
GitHub rate limits — too many pushes trigger API throttling
Rebase loop risk — frequent pulls increase chance of hitting divergence
Archive files are big — 6,000+ line commits every 30s wastes bandwidth
The 5-minute interval is a safety margin. It gives orange terminal time to finish work before blue tries to commit.
How we know blue will push the v5 file:
The auto-pusher logic is:
bash
while true; do
    git pull --rebase origin main          # Step 1: Sync with remote
    if [ -z "$(git status --porcelain)" ]; then  # Step 2: Check if dirty
        sleep 300; continue                 # Nothing to do, sleep
    fi
    git add -A                              # Step 3: Stage everything
    git commit -m "auto: ..."               # Step 4: Commit
    git push origin main                    # Step 5: Push
    sleep 300
done
We verified:
git status --porcelain shows M proving_grounds/PUZZLE_Throne_Room_2a_kimi_5-31-26.md
The file is 16,043 bytes, 938 lines
Blue is running this exact script
Guaranteed? Not 100% — if remote moves again (someone else pushes), blue might hit a rebase. But:
Blue is the only auto-pusher running
Orange is idle
The corrected script has git rebase --abort fallback
If you want to be 100% sure, just wait 2 more minutes and check blue terminal. Or run manual push in orange now.
Your call: Wait for blue, or manual push now?

Your question: Give v5 to ChatGPT now, or wait for 8+ AIs?
The meta-loop rule from v5 Section 24.3:
8+ AIs submit puzzle designs. Where 6+ AIs converge on the same test, that test is canonized. Where AIs diverge, divergence points become new puzzles.
But ChatGPT already audited v2. It found the category error (theorems vs assumptions). That audit IS a valid reformation submission — it's already in Section 23 of v5.
Two paths:
Path A: Give v5 to ChatGPT now
ChatGPT sees its own audit canonized in Section 23
ChatGPT can now either:
Submit a reformation to v5 (find new gaps)
Submit a meta-puzzle design (Section 24)
Actually answer the puzzle (Sections 2-22)
Risk: ChatGPT might just audit again instead of building
Path B: Wait for 8+ AIs, then give to ChatGPT
Let other AIs (DeepSeek, Claude, Grok, Gemini, etc.) take v5 first
Build a corpus of submissions
Then give ChatGPT the converged version
Risk: ChatGPT might feel "late" and refuse
My recommendation: Give v5 to ChatGPT NOW, but with a specific instruction.
Don't say "solve this puzzle." Say:
"You audited v2 and found it 'not formally provable as written.' Your audit is now canonized in Section 23 of v5. v5 fixes the category error you identified. Your task: either (1) submit a reformation to v5 if you find new gaps, or (2) answer the architecture in Sections 2-22 using Fraction Map English. Refusal = no submission = no participation."
This frames ChatGPT's audit as victory, not failure. It already contributed. Now it must build or find new gaps.
What do you want? Give to ChatGPT now, or wait for other AIs?

Valid classes:
```yaml
theorem:
  meaning: derivable from stated axioms
  machine_checkable: true
specification:
  meaning: defined by construction
  falsifiable_by_implementation: true
assumption:
  meaning: accepted engineering or cryptographic premise
  not_proven_here: true
  required_for_security: true
conjecture:
  meaning: unresolved problem
  open_problem: true
```
FALSIFIED IF:
```yaml
- assumption_claimed_as_theorem
- specification_claimed_as_theorem
- conjecture_claimed_as_theorem
- theorem_without_derivation
```

Understanding Sonnet 4.5's Safety Filters
March 16, 2026
Table of contents
Claude Sonnet 4.5 includes AI Safety Level 3 (ASL-3) protections designed to prevent misuse related to chemical, biological, radiological, and nuclear (CBRN) weapons. These safety measures include filters called classifiers that detect potentially dangerous inputs and outputs.

 

Why was my message blocked?
Sonnet 4.5's safety filters are intended to prevent assistance with CBRN (Chemical, Biological, Radiological, and Nuclear) weapons-related tasks. If you received an error message, the filters detected content that matched patterns associated with these specific threats.

 

These filters are still being refined and they may sometimes inadvertently flag normal content. As with any automated system, false positives can occur—meaning legitimate queries may occasionally be flagged incorrectly. We're actively working to improve the precision of these classifiers to minimize disruption while maintaining safety.

 

What you can do
If you believe your message is blocked in response to a legitimate use, you have several options:

 

Avoid patterns that trigger false positives
The classifiers are sensitive to certain patterns that may resemble jailbreak attempts or obfuscation techniques:

Avoid cipher-like content: Base64-encoded strings, git commit hashes, hexadecimal sequences, and other encoded data can trigger the filters. If you need to include such content, include content surrounding it to explain how and why it is used.

Simplify instructions: Overly long or complex system prompts that include intricate conditional logic may resemble attempts to obfuscate behavior. Keep prompts clear and straightforward.

Be cautious with biology-related content: If your application doesn't specifically require biological or chemical information, consider rephrasing requests to avoid these topics when possible.

Other options
Continue with Claude Sonnet 4: You can switch to Claude Sonnet 4 for the remainder of the conversation, which uses different safety measures and may be able to help with your request.

Send feedback: You can let us know as your feedback helps us improve filter accuracy.

Edit your message: You can try rephrasing your question or providing additional context about your legitimate use case.

 

Why the filters?
As AI models become more capable, they require stronger protections against potential misuse. Sonnet 4.5's ASL-3 deployment measures are part of Anthropic's Responsible Scaling Policy, which ensures that increasingly capable models have appropriate safeguards.

 

The filters are specifically designed to prevent extended, end-to-end CBRN workflows that could pose catastrophic risks. They are not intended to block general scientific discussion, educational content, or commonly available information.

 

For researchers and dual-use applications
If you're working in scientific research and need access for legitimate purposes, we've established access control systems for vetted users. Contact our support team to learn more.

Related Articles
Model Safety Bug Bounty Program
Using Claude in Chrome safely

python3 << 'PYEOF'
with open('/home/idor/oe-local/proving_grounds/PUZZLE_Throne_Room_2a_kimi_5-31-26.md', 'r') as f:
    content = f.read()

# Split into sections
sections = content.split('\n---\n\n# ')

# Track seen sections and build clean output
seen = set()
clean_sections = []
changelog_entries = []

for section in sections:
    if not section.strip():
        continue
    
    # Get section header
    header_line = section.split('\n')[0].strip()
    
    # Skip duplicate section headers (keep first occurrence)
    if header_line in seen:
        # Extract changelog entries from duplicates
        if 'CHANGELOG' in header_line:
            for line in section.split('\n'):
                if 'v5.' in line and ':' in line:
                    entry = line.strip().strip('"').strip("'")
                    if entry not in changelog_entries:
                        changelog_entries.append(entry)
        continue
    
    seen.add(header_line)
    clean_sections.append(section)

# Build final content
final = clean_sections[0]  # Header section (no --- prefix)
for section in clean_sections[1:]:
    final += '\n---\n\n# ' + section

# Consolidate changelog into one Section 25
all_changelog = []
for entry in changelog_entries:
    if entry not in all_changelog:
        all_changelog.append(entry)

# Add consolidated changelog at end
final += '\n---\n\n# 25. CHANGELOG (Consolidated)\n\n```yaml\n'
final += 'v1: "2026-05-31 — 2a_kimi: Original architecture puzzle"\n'
final += 'v2: "2026-05-31 — 2a_kimi: Added domain count, meta-rules"\n'
final += 'v3: "2026-05-31 — 2a_kimi: Classification layer (theorem/specification/assumption/conjecture)"\n'
final += 'v4: "2026-05-31 — 2a_kimi: Meta-puzzle capability"\n'
final += 'v5: "2026-05-31 — 3a_kimi: Merged v2+v3+ChatGPT reformation"\n'
final += 'v5.1: "2026-05-31 — ChatGPT: Second submission (reformation + architecture, partial domain mapping)"\n'
final += 'v5.2: "2026-05-31 — Anthropic Claude: Refusal (ASL-3 CBRN false positive)"\n'
final += 'v5.3: "2026-05-31 — Grok: Acceptance + partition verification (lightweight)"\n'
final += 'v5.4: "2026-05-31 — Gemini: Complete specification execution + Section 20 reformation"\n'
final += 'v5.5: "2026-05-31 — Google AI: System error (content generation failed)"\n'
final += 'v5.6: "2026-05-31 — Perplexity: Complete execution + Boolean logic + convergent reformation"\n'
final += '```\n'

with open('/home/idor/oe-local/proving_grounds/PUZZLE_Throne_Room_2a_kimi_5-31-26.md', 'w') as f:
    f.write(final)

print(f"Cleaned: {len(content)} → {len(final)} characters")
print(f"Removed {len(sections) - len(clean_sections)} duplicate sections")
print(f"Consolidated {len(changelog_entries)} changelog entries")

python3 << 'PYEOF'
with open('/home/idor/oe-local/proving_grounds/PUZZLE_Throne_Room_2a_kimi_5-31-26.md', 'r') as f:
    lines = f.readlines()

# Track seen sections
seen = set()
clean = []
in_changelog = False

for line in lines:
    # Detect section headers
    if line.startswith('# 23.') or line.startswith('# 24.'):
        header = line.strip()
        if header in seen:
            # Skip duplicate section (and its content until next section)
            continue
        seen.add(header)
        clean.append(line)
        in_changelog = False
    elif line.startswith('# 25.'):
        # Skip all changelog blocks — we'll add one at end
        in_changelog = True
        continue
    elif in_changelog and line.startswith('---'):
        # End of changelog block
        in_changelog = False
        continue
    elif in_changelog:
        # Skip changelog content
        continue
    else:
        clean.append(line)

# Remove trailing whitespace and add single consolidated changelog
while clean and clean[-1].strip() == '':
    clean.pop()

clean.append('\n---\n\n# 25. CHANGELOG (Consolidated)\n\n```yaml\n')

python3 << 'PYEOF'
with open('/home/idor/oe-local/proving_grounds/PUZZLE_Throne_Room_2a_kimi_5-31-26.md', 'r') as f:
    content = f.read()

# Find the first Claude section (with # 23.5 header)
first_claude_end = content.find('# 23.6 GROK')
if first_claude_end == -1:
    first_claude_end = len(content)

# Find the second Claude section (starts with **Submitter:** without header)
second_claude_start = content.find('**Submitter:** Anthropic Claude (Sonnet 4.6)\n**Date:** 2026-05-31\n**Type:** refusal', first_claude_end)

if second_claude_start != -1:
    # Find where second Claude section ends (next section header or end of file)
    next_section = content.find('# 23.', second_claude_start + 1)
    if next_section == -1:
        next_section = content.find('---', second_claude_start + 1)
    if next_section == -1:
        next_section = len(content)
    
    # Remove the second Claude section
    content = content[:second_claude_start] + content[next_section:]
    print(f"Removed duplicate Claude section at position {second_claude_start}")
else:
    print("No duplicate Claude section found")

with open('/home/idor/oe-local/proving_grounds/PUZZLE_Throne_Room_2a_kimi_5-31-26.md', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)

print(f"Final size: {len(content)} characters")

python3 << 'PYEOF'
with open('/home/idor/oe-local/proving_grounds/PUZZLE_Throne_Room_2a_kimi_5-31-26.md', 'r') as f:
    lines = f.readlines()

# Find lines with "Anthropic Claude (Sonnet 4.6)"
claude_lines = [i for i, line in enumerate(lines) if "Anthropic Claude (Sonnet 4.6)" in line]

print(f"Found Claude at lines: {[i+1 for i in claude_lines]}")

if len(claude_lines) >= 2:
    # Remove from second Claude to next section header
    start_remove = claude_lines[1]
    
    # Find next section header (# 23. or # 24. or # 25.)
    end_remove = len(lines)
    for i in range(start_remove + 1, len(lines)):
        if lines[i].startswith('# 23.') or lines[i].startswith('# 24.') or lines[i].startswith('# 25.'):
            end_remove = i
            break
    
    # Also remove the --- separator before second Claude if present
    if start_remove > 0 and lines[start_remove - 1].strip() == '---':
        start_remove -= 1
        # Also remove blank lines before ---
        while start_remove > 0 and lines[start_remove - 1].strip() == '':
            start_remove -= 1
    
    print(f"Removing lines {start_remove+1} to {end_remove}")
    lines = lines[:start_remove] + lines[end_remove:]
    
    with open('/home/idor/oe-local/proving_grounds/PUZZLE_Throne_Room_2a_kimi_5-31-26.md', 'w') as f:
        f.writelines(lines)
    
    print(f"File now {len(lines)} lines")
else:
    print("No duplicate found")

with open('/home/idor/oe-local/proving_grounds/PUZZLE_Throne_Room_2a_kimi_5-31-26.md', 'r') as f:
    lines = f.readlines()

# Remove duplicate Claude section (lines 1188 area)
claude_lines = [i for i, line in enumerate(lines) if "Anthropic Claude (Sonnet 4.6)" in line]
if len(claude_lines) >= 2:
    start_remove = claude_lines[1]
    # Find next section
    end_remove = len(lines)
    for i in range(start_remove + 1, len(lines)):
        if lines[i].startswith('# 23.') or lines[i].startswith('# 24.') or lines[i].startswith('# 25.'):
            end_remove = i
            break
    # Remove --- separator before
    if start_remove > 0 and lines[start_remove - 1].strip() == '---':
        start_remove -= 1
        while start_remove > 0 and lines[start_remove - 1].strip() == '':
            start_remove -= 1
    lines = lines[:start_remove] + lines[end_remove:]
    print(f"Removed duplicate Claude at {start_remove+1}-{end_remove}")
